exists if and only if

(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}=k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}>(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}=k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-},

•

the dotted arrow between
k+3
and
k+2
exists if and only if

(k+3)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{+}=(k+2)_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{+}<(k+3)_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{+}=(k+2)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{+}.

Thus our assertion follows. For
C_{n}
and
F_{4}
-cases, one can prove in a similar way.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

We have

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}=\sigma_{k+2}\sigma_{k}\mu_{k}\mu_{k+1}\mu_{k}\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}}.

Proof.

Let us denote by

\Lambda\mathbin{:=}\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}\mathbin{:=}\mu_{k}\mu_{k+1}\mu_{k}\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}}\qquad\text{for notational simplicity.}

Note that

\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u,v}=\Lambda_{u,v}\quad\text{ if }u,v\not\in\{k,k+1\}.

(A) For the cases when
|\{u\neq v\}\cap\{k,k+1\}|=1
, we have

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k+1,v}

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{\prime\prime}_{k+1,v}=-\Lambda_{k+1,v}+\Lambda_{k+3,v}+\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},v}\quad(\text{for }v>k+1),

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u,k+1}

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{\prime\prime}_{u,k+1}=-\Lambda_{u,k+1}+\Lambda_{u,k+3}+\Lambda_{u,(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}}\quad(\text{for }u<k),

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k,v}

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{k,v}+\mathsf{c}_{i_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}},i_{k}}\Lambda_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},v}-\Lambda_{k+2,v}-\mathsf{c}_{i_{k+3},i_{k}}\Lambda_{k+3,v}+\Lambda_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},v}\quad(\text{for }v>k+1),

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u,k}

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{u,k}+\mathsf{c}_{i_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}},i_{k}}\Lambda_{u,(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}-\Lambda_{u,k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{i_{k+3},i_{k}}\Lambda_{u,k+3}+\Lambda_{u,k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}}\quad(\text{for }u<k),

by direct calculations.

(B) For the cases when
\{u,v\}=\{k,k+1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k,k+1}

\displaystyle=-\Lambda_{k,k+1}-\mathsf{c}_{i_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}},i_{k}}\Lambda_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},k+1}-\Lambda_{k+1,k+2}+\Lambda_{k,k+3}+\mathsf{c}_{i_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}},i_{k}}\Lambda_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},k+3}

\displaystyle-\Lambda_{k+2,k+3}-\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k}+\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{i_{k+3},i_{k}}\Lambda_{k+1,k+3}+\mathsf{c}_{i_{k+3},i_{k}}\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+3}

\displaystyle-\Lambda_{k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+1}+\Lambda_{k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+3}+\Lambda_{k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}},

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k+1,k}

\displaystyle=-\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k,k+1}.

Recall that
(i_{k},i_{k+1},i_{k+2},i_{k+3})=(i,j,i,j)\overset{\eta_{k}}{\leadsto}(i^{\prime}_{k},i^{\prime}_{k+1},i^{\prime}_{k+2},i^{\prime}_{k+3})=(j,i,j,i)
.
Thus it suffices to prove that the following equation holds:

\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\sigma_{k+2}\sigma_{k}(u),\sigma_{k+2}\sigma_{k}(v)}=\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{u,v}.

Since only
\{k,k+1,k+2,k+3\}
are changed by
\sigma_{k+2}\sigma_{k}
, it is enough to to show that the following
15
equations hold:

(i)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k,v}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k+1,v}=-\Lambda_{k+1,v}+\Lambda_{k+3,v}+\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},v}
for
v>k+3
,

(ii)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+1,v}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k,v}=\Lambda_{k,v}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},v}-\Lambda_{k+2,v}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{k+3,v}+\Lambda_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},v}
for
v>k+3
,

(iii)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+2,v}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k+3,v}=\Lambda_{k+3,v}
for
v>k+3
,

(iv)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+3,v}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k+2,v}=\Lambda_{k+2,v}
for
v>k+3
,

(v)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{u,k}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u,k+1}=-\Lambda_{u,k+1}+\Lambda_{u,k+3}+\Lambda_{u,(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}}
for
u<k
,

(vi)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{u,k+1}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u,k}=\Lambda_{u,k}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{u,(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}-\Lambda_{u,k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{u,k+3}+\Lambda_{u,k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}}
for
u<k
,

(vii)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{u,k+2}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u,k+3}=\Lambda_{u,k+3}
for
u<k
,

(viii)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{u,k+3}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u,k+2}=\Lambda_{u,k+2}
for
u<k
,

(ix)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k,k+1}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k+1,k}
,

(x)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+2,k+3}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k+3,k+2}=\Lambda_{k+3,k+2}
,

(xi)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k,k+3}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k+1,k+2}=-\Lambda_{k+1,k+2}+\Lambda_{k+3,k+2}+\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+2}
,

(xii)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k,k+2}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k+1,k+3}=-\Lambda_{k+1,k+3}+\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+3}
,

(xiii)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+1,k+2}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k,k+3}=\Lambda_{k,k+3}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},k+3}-\Lambda_{k+2,k+3}+\Lambda_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},k+3}
,

(xiv)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+1,k+3}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k,k+2}=\Lambda_{k,k+2}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{k+3,k+2}+\Lambda_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},k+2}
,

(xv)

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{u,v}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u,v}
for
u,v\not\in\{k,k+1,k+2,k+3\}
.

(
iii
), (
iv
), (
vii
), (
viii
) and (
x
)-cases: Let us recall the following facts:

(a)

w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k+3}\varpi_{j}=w^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+3}\varpi_{j}=w^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+2}\varpi_{j}
,

(b)

w^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+3}\varpi_{i}=w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k+3}\varpi_{i}=w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k+2}\varpi_{i}.

The assertion for (
iii
), (
iv
), (
vii
) and (
viii
)-cases follows directly. Let us check (
x
)-cases. By Lemma
3.2
, we have

\displaystyle\Lambda_{k+3,k+2}

\displaystyle=(\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k+3}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{i}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k+2}\varpi_{i})

\displaystyle=(\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+2}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{i}+w^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+3}\varpi_{i})=\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+3,k+2},

which complete our assertions for these cases.

(
i
), (
v
), (
xi
) and (
xii
)-cases: Since the proofs for these cases are all similar, we shall give a proof for (
i
)-case only.
Note that

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k,v}=(\varpi_{j}-w_{k-1}^{\textbf{{i}}}s_{j}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}})=(\varpi_{j}-w_{k-1}^{\textbf{{i}}}(\varpi_{j}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}}).

On the other hand

\displaystyle-\Lambda_{k+1,v}

\displaystyle=-(\varpi_{j}-w_{k-1}^{\textbf{{i}}}s_{i}s_{j}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}})

\displaystyle=(-\varpi_{j}-w_{k-1}^{\textbf{{i}}}(-\varpi_{j}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}),\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}}),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{k+3,v}

\displaystyle=(\varpi_{j}-w_{k-1}^{\textbf{{i}}}s_{i}s_{j}s_{i}s_{j}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}})

\displaystyle=(\varpi_{j}-w_{k-1}^{\textbf{{i}}}(\varpi_{j}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}-2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}}),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},v}

\displaystyle=(\varpi_{j}-w_{k-1}^{\textbf{{i}}}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}}).

Thus the sum of the three given above is

(\varpi_{j}-w_{k-1}^{\textbf{{i}}}(\varpi_{j}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}})=\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k,v},

which shows the assertion (
i
).

(
ii
), (
vi
), (
xiii
) and (
xiv
)-cases:
Since the proofs for these cases are all similar, we shall give a proof for (
ii
)-case only.
Note that

\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+1,v}=(\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{j}s_{i}\varpi_{i},\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}})=(\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}(\varpi_{i}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}}).

First let us consider the following two terms:

\displaystyle\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},v}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}})

\displaystyle-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{k+3,v}

\displaystyle=(-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}(-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\varpi_{j}-2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}+2\mathsf{c}_{j,i}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j})\varpi_{j},\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}}),

whose sum is

(3.12)

\displaystyle(-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}(-2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}+2\mathsf{c}_{j,i}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),\varpi_{\textbf{{i}}_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}}).

Next, let us consider the following three terms:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{k,v}

\displaystyle=(\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}\varpi_{i},\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}})=(\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}(\varpi_{i}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}),\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}})

\displaystyle-\Lambda_{k+2,v}

\displaystyle=-(\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}s_{i}\varpi_{i},\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}})

\displaystyle=(-\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}(-\varpi_{i}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}),\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}}),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},v}

\displaystyle=(\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}\varpi_{i},\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}}),

whose sum is

(3.13)

\displaystyle(\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}(\varpi_{i}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}),\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}}).

Hence, RHS of (
ii
) becomes

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: (ii)-step1}+\eqref{eq: (ii)-step2}

\displaystyle=(\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}(\varpi_{i}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),\varpi_{i_{v}}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}})=\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k+1,v},

which implies assertion (
ii
).

(
ix
)-case: This is the most complicated case. We have to show that

(3.14)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k,k+1}

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{k,k+1}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},k+1}+\Lambda_{k+1,k+2}-\Lambda_{k,k+3}

\displaystyle-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},k+3}+\Lambda_{k+2,k+3}+\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k}-\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+2}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{k+1,k+3}

\displaystyle-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+3}+\Lambda_{k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+1}-\Lambda_{k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+3}-\Lambda_{k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}},

whose RHS consists of
13
terms.

On the other hand,
\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k,k+1}
can be rewritten as follows:

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k,k+1}

\displaystyle=(\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{j}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{i}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{j}s_{i}\varpi_{i})

\displaystyle=(\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}(\varpi_{j}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),\varpi_{i}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}(\varpi_{i}-{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}))

\displaystyle=(\varpi_{j},w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}(\varpi_{i}))-(\varpi_{j},w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}))+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(\varpi_{j},w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}))

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt-(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}(\varpi_{j}),\varpi_{i})+(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),\varpi_{i}).

Let us analyze the
13
terms in (
3.14
):

(1)

\Lambda_{k,k+1}=-\Lambda_{k+1,k}\overset{Lemma~\ref{Lem:Lambda}}{=}-(\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{i}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}\varpi_{i}),

(2)

\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k}=(\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{i}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}\varpi_{i})
,

(3)

\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},k+1}=\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{j}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}\varpi_{j})
,

(4)

-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},k+3}=-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{j}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}s_{i}s_{j}\varpi_{j})
,

(5)

\Lambda_{k+1,k+2}=(\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{i}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}s_{i}\varpi_{i}),

(6)

-\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+2}=-(\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{i}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}s_{i}\varpi_{i})
,

(7)

-\Lambda_{k,k+3}=-(\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}\varpi_{i},\varpi_{j}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}s_{i}s_{j}\varpi_{j}),

(8)

\Lambda_{k+2,k+3}=(\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}s_{i}\varpi_{i},\varpi_{j}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}s_{i}s_{j}\varpi_{j})
,

(9)

\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{k+1,k+3}=\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{j}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}s_{i}s_{j}\varpi_{j})
,

(10)

-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\Lambda_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+3}=-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(\varpi_{j}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}\varpi_{j},\varpi_{j}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}s_{i}s_{j}\varpi_{j})
,

(11)

\Lambda_{k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+1}=(\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}\varpi_{i},\varpi_{j}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}\varpi_{j})
,

(12)

-\Lambda_{k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},k+3}=-(\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}\varpi_{i},\varpi_{j}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}s_{j}s_{i}s_{j}\varpi_{j})
,

(13)

-\Lambda_{k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}}\overset{Lemma~\ref{Lem:Lambda}}{=}-(\varpi_{i}-w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}\varpi_{i},\varpi_{j}+w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}\varpi_{j})
,

Using the techniques applied to the previous cases, we have

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: (1)}+\eqref{eq: (7)}

\displaystyle=-(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),\varpi_{i}),

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: (2)}+\eqref{eq: (5)}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(\varpi_{j},w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}))-({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: (3)}+\eqref{eq: (8)}

\displaystyle=({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i})+(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}s_{i}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),\varpi_{i}),

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: (4)}+\eqref{eq: (6)}

\displaystyle=(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),\varpi_{j})-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},\varpi_{j})+({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: (9)}+\eqref{eq: (10)}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j},\varpi_{j})-2(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i}),\varpi_{j})-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{i},\varpi_{i}),

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: (11)}+\eqref{eq: (12)}+\eqref{eq: (13)}

\displaystyle=-(\varpi_{i},w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}(\varpi_{j}))+(\varpi_{i},w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}))+(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}\varpi_{i},\varpi_{j}).

Hence the summation of the
13
terms becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{13}(s)

\displaystyle=-(\varpi_{i},w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}(\varpi_{j}))+(\varpi_{i},w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}))+(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}\varpi_{i},\varpi_{j})

\displaystyle\hskip 64.58313pt-(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}),\varpi_{j})+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(\varpi_{j},w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k-1}({\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{j}))=\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{k,k+1},

which completes a proof of assertion (
ix
).
∎

As a consequence of the previous two lemmas, we have the following proposition:

Proposition 3.8
.

We have an isomorphism of
\mathbb{A}
-algebras

\eta_{k}^{*}(=\mu_{k}^{*}\mu_{k+1}^{*}\mu_{k}^{*}\sigma_{k}^{*}\sigma_{k+2}^{*})\colon\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}

given by

Z_{u}\mapsto\mu_{k}^{*}\mu_{k+1}^{*}\mu_{k}^{*}(Z_{\sigma_{k+2}\sigma_{k}(u)})\quad\text{for all $u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}$},

which induces a bijection between the sets of quantum cluster monomials.

Lemma 3.9
.

If
\mathsf{z}\in\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}
is a quantum cluster monomial with
\mathbf{deg}(\mathsf{z})={\bm{g}}^{\prime}=(g^{\prime}_{u})_{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
, then the quantum cluster monomial

\eta_{k}^{*}\mathsf{z}\in\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}
has
\mathbf{deg}(\eta_{k}^{*}\mathsf{z})={\bm{g}}=(g_{u})_{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
as follows:

(3.15)

\displaystyle g_{u}=\begin{cases}g_{k+2}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}+[A]_{+}&\text{ if }u=k+3,\\
g_{k+3}^{\prime}-[-g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}+[B]_{+}&\text{ if }u=k+2,\\
-A+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}&\text{ if }u=k+1,\\
-B&\text{ if }u=k,\\
g_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}}^{\prime}+[g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}-[-B]_{+}&\text{ if }u=k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},\\
g_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}}^{\prime}+[A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}&\text{ if }u=(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},\\
g_{u}^{\prime}&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}

where

(3.16)

\displaystyle A=g_{k}^{\prime}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}\quad\text{ and }\quad B=-g_{k+1}^{\prime}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-A]_{+}.

Proof.

By applying (
2.4
) repeatedly,
we obtain the assertion.
∎

Lemma 3.10
.

The map
{\bm{g}}^{\prime}\mapsto{\bm{g}}
given by (
3.15
) sends the cone
C_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}\subset\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}^{\oplus\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
into the cone
C_{\textbf{{i}}}\subset\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}^{\oplus\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
.

Proof.

To simplify the notation, for
{\bm{g}}=(g_{u})_{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
and
{\bm{g}}^{\prime}=(g^{\prime}_{u})_{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
, we set

(3.17)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{u}\mathbin{:=}\sum_{v\geqslant u,i_{v}=i_{u}}g_{v},\quad\Sigma^{\prime}_{u}\mathbin{:=}\sum_{v\geqslant u,i^{\prime}_{v}=i^{\prime}_{u}}g^{\prime}_{v}

for each
u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}
. Throughout this proof, we shall use the notations
A
and
B
in (
3.16
).

We have to show that
\Sigma^{\prime}_{u}\geqslant 0

(\forall u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu})
implies
\Sigma_{u}\geqslant 0

(\forall u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu})
.

(a)

If
u>k+3
, we have
\Sigma_{u}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{u}\geqslant 0
.

(b)

If
u=k+3
, we have
\Sigma_{k+3}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}+[A]_{+}\geqslant 0
.

(c)

If
u=k+2
, the assertion holds when
g^{\prime}_{k+1}\geqslant 0
obviously. Let us assume further
g^{\prime}_{k+1}<0
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+2}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+3}+g_{k+1}^{\prime}+[B]_{+}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+1}+[B]_{+},

which implies the assertion for
u=k+2
.

(d)

If
u=k+1
, we have

(3.18)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}+[A]_{+}-A+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}+[-A]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}.

Thus the assertion holds when
B\geqslant 0
. Assume that
B<0
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}([g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}-g^{\prime}_{k+1})+[-A]_{+}-2[-A]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}-[-A]_{+}.

Thus the assertion holds when
B<0
and
A>0
. Let us consider the case when
B<0
and
A<0
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}-A-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}B

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}-A+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g_{k+1}^{\prime}+2A

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g_{k+1}^{\prime}+g_{k}^{\prime}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k},

which completes the assertion.

(e)

If
u=k
, we have

(3.19)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k}

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+1}-[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+[-B]_{+}

\displaystyle=\delta(g^{\prime}_{k+1}\geqslant 0)\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+3}+\delta(g^{\prime}_{k+1}<0)\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+1}+[-B]_{+}\geqslant 0,

which implies the assertion.

(f)

If
u<k
, we have

\displaystyle g_{k+3}+g_{k+1}+g_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}

\displaystyle=g_{k+2}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}+2[A]_{+}-A+g_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}B

\displaystyle=g_{k+2}^{\prime}+g_{k}^{\prime}+g_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}}^{\prime}

and

\displaystyle g_{k+2}+g_{k}+g_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}=g_{k+3}^{\prime}+g_{k+1}^{\prime}+g_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}}^{\prime},

and thus
\Sigma_{u}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{u}
, which implies the assertion. ∎

By (
f
) in the proof of Lemma
3.10
, we have the following corollary:

Corollary 3.11
.

Assume that
k^{-}\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}
. Then we have

\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{g}}^{\prime};i)=\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{g}};i)\quad\text{ for all }i\in I,

where
{\bm{g}}^{\prime}\mapsto{\bm{g}}
under the map given by (
3.15
).

Lemma 3.12
.

The elements
{\bm{g}},{\bm{g}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}^{\oplus\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
related by (
3.15
) satisfy
the followings:

(1)

When
i=i_{k+1}
and
(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}=0
, we have

\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{g}};i)=\begin{cases}\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{g}}^{\prime};i)-[g_{k}^{\prime}]_{+}&\text{ if }g_{k+1}^{\prime}\geqslant 0,\\
\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{g}}^{\prime};i)-g_{k}^{\prime}&\text{ if }g_{k+1}^{\prime}<0,g_{k}^{\prime}\geqslant 0\text{ and }A\geqslant 0,\\
\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{g}}^{\prime};i)-2g_{k}^{\prime}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g_{k+1}^{\prime}&\text{ if }g_{k+1}^{\prime}<0,g_{k}^{\prime}\geqslant 0,A<0\text{ and }B\geqslant 0,\\
\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{g}}^{\prime};i)&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

(2)

When
i=i_{k}
and
k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}=0
, we have

\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{g}};i)=\begin{cases}\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{g}}^{\prime};i)-g^{\prime}_{k+1}&\text{ if }g_{k+1}^{\prime}\geqslant 0,B\geqslant 0,\\
\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{g}}^{\prime};i)-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-g_{k}^{\prime}]_{+}&\text{ if }g_{k+1}^{\prime}\geqslant 0,B<0,\\
\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{g}}^{\prime};i)&\text{ if }g_{k+1}^{\prime}<0,B\geqslant 0,\\
\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{g}}^{\prime};i)-g_{k+1}^{\prime}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-g_{k}^{\prime}]_{+}&\text{ if }g_{k+1}^{\prime}<0,B<0.\end{cases}

(3)

Except above two cases, we have

\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{g}};i)=\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{g}}^{\prime};i).

Here

A=g_{k}^{\prime}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}\quad\text{ and }\quad B=-g_{k+1}^{\prime}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-A]_{+}.

Proof.

Since the proofs for
\gamma_{k}
and
\beta_{k}
are given in
[
11
, Lemma 2.18]
, let us focus on the case
\eta_{k}
.

(1) Let us first consider when
i=i_{k+1}
and
(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}=0
. By Corollary
3.11
, we need to focus on the case when

(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}=0
.
In (
3.18
), we see that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+1}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}+[-A]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}.

(i) If
g_{k+1}^{\prime}\geqslant 0
, then
A=g_{k}^{\prime}
,
[-B]_{+}=g^{\prime}_{k+1}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-g_{k}^{\prime}]_{+}
and

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g_{k+1}^{\prime}+[-g_{k}^{\prime}]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(g^{\prime}_{k+1}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-g_{k}^{\prime}]_{+})

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}-[-g_{k}^{\prime}]_{+}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k}-[g_{k}^{\prime}]_{+}.

(ii) If
g_{k+1}^{\prime}<0
, then
A=g_{k}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g_{k+1}^{\prime}
and
B=-g_{k+1}^{\prime}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-A]_{+}.

(ii-a) Let us assume that
g_{k}^{\prime}<0
and
A\leqslant 0
. Then
B=g_{k+1}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}g_{k}^{\prime}<0
and

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g_{k+1}^{\prime}]_{+}+[-A]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}+g_{k}^{\prime}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k}

(ii-b) Let us assume that
g_{k}^{\prime}\geqslant 0
and
A\geqslant 0
. Then

B=-g_{k+1}^{\prime}
and

\Sigma_{k+1}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k}-g_{k}^{\prime}.

(ii-c) Finally, assume that
g_{k}^{\prime}\geqslant 0
and
A<0
. Then

B=g_{k+1}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}g_{k}^{\prime}
.

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+2}-g_{k}^{\prime}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g_{k+1}^{\prime}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}=\begin{cases}\Sigma^{\prime}_{k}-2g_{k}^{\prime}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g_{k+1}^{\prime}&\text{ if }B\geqslant 0,\\
\Sigma^{\prime}_{k}&\text{ if }B<0.\end{cases}

(2) Let us consider when
i=i_{k}
.
In (
3.19
), we see that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+1}-[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+[-B]_{+}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k}=\begin{cases}\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+1}-g^{\prime}_{k+1}&\text{ if }g_{k+1}^{\prime}\geqslant 0,B\geqslant 0,\\
\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+1}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-g_{k}^{\prime}]_{+}&\text{ if }g_{k+1}^{\prime}\geqslant 0,B<0,\\
\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+1}&\text{ if }g_{k+1}^{\prime}<0,B\geqslant 0,\\
\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+1}-g_{k+1}^{\prime}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-g_{k}^{\prime}]_{+}&\text{ if }g_{k+1}^{\prime}<0,B<0,\end{cases}

which implies our assertion.
∎

3.6.
6
-moves

For sequences
\textbf{{i}}=(i_{u})_{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
and
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=(i^{\prime}_{u})_{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}\in I^{(\infty)}
,
we say that
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}
can be obtained from
i
via a
6
-move
, if there
exists a unique
k\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}
such that

\displaystyle i_{k}=i_{k+2}=i_{k+4}=i^{\prime}_{k+1}=i^{\prime}_{k+3}=i^{\prime}_{k+5},

\displaystyle i_{k+1}=i_{k+3}=i_{k+5}=i^{\prime}_{k}=i^{\prime}_{k+2}=i^{\prime}_{k+4},

\displaystyle i_{u}=i^{\prime}_{u}\ (u\not\in[k,k+5])

and

\mathsf{c}_{i_{k+1},i_{k}}\mathsf{c}_{i_{k},i_{k+1}}=3.

In this case, we write
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=\zeta_{k}\textbf{{i}}
.
Throughout this subsection, we assume that
\textbf{{i}},\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}\in I^{(\infty)}
are related by
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=\zeta_{k}\textbf{{i}}
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}

and we sometimes write
i\mathbin{:=}i_{k}
and
j\mathbin{:=}i_{k+1}
.

Lemma 3.13
.

We have

(3.20)

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}=\sigma_{k+4}\sigma_{k+2}\sigma_{k}\mu_{k}\mu_{k+3}\mu_{k+2}\mu_{k}\mu_{k+1}\mu_{k+3}\mu_{k}\mu_{k+2}\mu_{k+1}\mu_{k}\widetilde{B}^{\textbf{{i}}}.

Proof.

As in the
4
-move case, it is enough to show that

\mu_{k}\mu_{k+3}\mu_{k+2}\mu_{k}\mu_{k+1}\mu_{k+3}\mu_{k}\mu_{k+2}\mu_{k+1}\mu_{k}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\textbf{{i}}}|_{\mathcal{K}}=\sigma_{k+4}\sigma_{k+2}\sigma_{k}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}|_{\mathcal{K}}

where
\mathcal{K}=[k,k+5]\sqcup\{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}},(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}\}
.

We shall only consider the case when both
k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}
and
(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}
are nonzero since the proofs for the other cases are similar.

Lemma 3.14
.

We have

(3.25)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}=\sigma_{k+4}\sigma_{k+2}\sigma_{k}\mu_{k}\mu_{k+3}\mu_{k+2}\mu_{k}\mu_{k+1}\mu_{k+3}\mu_{k}\mu_{k+2}\mu_{k+1}\mu_{k}\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}}.

Proof.

To simplify notation, let us denote
\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}}
as
\Lambda
. Unless
k\leq u\leq k+5
or
k\leq v\leq k+5
, the expressions
\Lambda_{u,v}=\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}_{u,v}
hold true, and the values of
\Lambda_{u,v}
remain unchanged under the operations
\mu_{k}
,
\mu_{k+1}
,
\mu_{k+2}
,
\mu_{k+3}
,
\sigma_{k}
,
\sigma_{k+2}
, and
\sigma_{k+4}
. Therefore, for simplicity, we will only consider
\Lambda_{u,v}
when
k\leq u\leq k+5
or
k\leq v\leq k+5
, which we will assume from now on. Furthermore, due to the skew-symmetry of
\Lambda
and
\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}
, we can assume further
u<v
.

Recall the definition

\Lambda_{u,v}=(\varpi_{i_{u}}-w_{u}\varpi_{i_{u}},\varpi_{i_{v}}+w_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}})\quad\text{for $u<v$}.

Since the number of elements in the set
\mathsf{W}
is 12 and the sequence
i
contains only
1
and
2
, we will demonstrate that there exist only a limited number of finite subsequences of
i
that need to be examined to directly verify the assertion.

Consider a fixed set
\underline{\mathsf{W}}
consisting of reduced sequences of elements in
\mathsf{W}
. Let us examine how
\Lambda_{u,v}
is determined. To avoid minor exceptions, assume
k\geqslant 4
. If
u<k
and
k\leqslant v\leqslant k+5
, then
\Lambda_{u,v}
is determined by the subsequence

w_{1}\,a\,w_{2}\,i_{k}\,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{k+5},

where
w_{1},w_{2}\in\underline{\mathsf{W}}
,
i_{u}=a
, and
i_{v}=i_{\ell}
for some
\ell=k,k+1,\dots,k+5
. Note that it is sufficient to consider
w_{1},w_{2}\in\underline{\mathsf{W}}
, as
\Lambda_{u,v}
does not change with other expressions of the Weyl group elements represented by
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
.

If
k\leqslant u,v\leqslant k+5
then
\Lambda_{u,v}
is determined by

i_{k}\,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{k+5}.

If
k\leqslant u\leqslant k+5
and
v>k+5
then
\Lambda_{u,v}
is determined by the subsequence

i_{k}\,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{k+5}w_{3}d,

where
w_{3}\in\underline{\mathsf{W}}
,
i_{v}=d
,
i_{u}=i_{\ell}
for some
\ell=k,k+1,\dots,k+5
.

All three cases mentioned above are encompassed in the sequences given by

(3.26)

{w_{1}}\,a\,{w_{2}}\,i_{k}\,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{k+5}\,{w_{3}}\,d,

where
w_{1},w_{2},w_{3}\in\underline{\mathsf{W}}
,
a,d\in\{1,2\}
and

(i_{k},i_{k+1},\dots,i_{k+5})\in\{(1,2,1,2,1,2),(2,1,2,1,2,1)\}.

However, to apply
\mu_{k},\mu_{k+1},\mu_{k+2},\mu_{k+3}
, it is crucial to keep track of the corresponding mutations of
\widetilde{B}^{\textbf{{i}}}
. As demonstrated in the proof of Lemma
3.13
, we only need to consider
k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}
and
(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}
in addition to
k,k+1,\dots,k+5
when dealing with mutations (note that
(k+4)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{+}
and
(k+5)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{+}
are not relevant). In the matrices obtained from the mutations of
\widetilde{B}^{\textbf{{i}}}
associated with the mutations of
\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}}
in the statement of this lemma, we observe that

(3.27)

the
(k,j)
- and
(k+1,j)
-entries are zero for
j<k
unless
j=k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}
or
j=(k+1)\textbf{{i}}^{-}
.

Therefore, these entries do not contribute to the mutations of
\Lambda_{u,v}
by definition.

Now consider a sequence

\textbf{{j}}_{1}a\textbf{{j}}_{2}\,i_{k}\,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{k+5}\,\textbf{{j}}_{3}d,

where
\textbf{{j}}_{1}=i_{1}\,i_{2}\cdots i_{u-1}
,
i_{u}=a
,
i_{v}=d
and
\textbf{{j}}_{2}
and
\textbf{{j}}_{3}
are finite sequences.
Let
\{b,c\}=\{i_{k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}},i_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-}}\}
. Without loss of generality, we may consider the following three cases:

\displaystyle\text{ (i) $\textbf{{j}}_{1}a\cdots bc\cdots c\,i_{k}\,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{k+5}\,\textbf{{j}}_{3}d$},

\displaystyle\text{(ii) $\textbf{{j}}_{1}ac\cdots c\,i_{k}\,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{k+5}\,\textbf{{j}}_{3}d$ \qquad with $a=b$},

\displaystyle\text{(iii) $\cdots ba\cdots a\,i_{k}\,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{k+5}\,\textbf{{j}}_{3}d$ \qquad with $a=c$ }.

Applying the observations (
3.26
) and (
3.27
) in determining
\Lambda_{u,v}
and their mutations, we may only to consider

(3.28)

\displaystyle\text{ (i) $\leadsto\ w_{1}aw_{2}bc\,i_{k}\,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{k+5}\,w_{3}d$},

(3.29)

\displaystyle\text{(ii) $\leadsto\ w_{1}ac\,i_{k}\,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{k+5}\,w_{3}d\quad\text{ or }\quad w_{1}acc\,i_{k}\,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{k+5}\,w_{3}d$},

(3.30)

\displaystyle\text{(iii) $\leadsto\ w_{1}a\,i_{k}\,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{k+5}\,w_{3}d$ },

where
w_{1},w_{2},w_{3}\in\underline{\mathsf{W}}
,
a,d\in\{1,2\}
,
(b,c)\in\{(1,2),(2,1)\}
and

(i_{k},i_{k+1},\dots,i_{k+5})\in\{(1,2,1,2,1,2),(2,1,2,1,2,1)\}.

We consider the submatrices of
\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}}
given by the finite sequences in (
3.28
)-(
3.30
).
These sequences are not mutually exclusive, and we deliberately maintain this redundancy for convenience, as it is unnecessary to remove it when directly checking using a computer.
The total number of sequences in (
3.28
)-(
3.30
) is 62,208. If
k\leqslant 3
, it is easy to list all possible sequences. We have used a computer to verify the
assertion of the lemma for all these finite subsequences, which took less than 12 minutes on a regular laptop (single CPU).
∎

Remark 3.15
.

The above lemma assumes a general situation. For our purpose in later sections, we need only special
i

and
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}
in this remark (see Remark
5.22
below also),
and hence we
do not
need to use a computer.
Consider
\textbf{{i}}=(1,2,1,2,1,2,1,2,\ldots)
and
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=(1,2,2,1,2,1,2,1,\ldots)
with
k=3
. Then we obtain

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}},8}={\tiny\left(\begin{array}[]{rrrrrrrr}0&-3&-1&-3&0&0&2&3\\
3&0&0&-3&0&0&3&6\\
1&0&0&-3&0&0&3&6\\

\displaystyle{\tiny\left(\begin{array}[]{rrrrrrrr}0&-3&-1&-3&0&0&2&3\\

-3&-6&0&0&-3&-3&-3&0\end{array}\right)}\xrightarrow{\mu_{3}\mu_{6}\mu_{5}}

\displaystyle{\tiny\left(\begin{array}[]{rrrrrrrr}0&-3&2&0&3&3&2&3\\

-2&-3&0&0&1&3&0&3\\
-3&-6&0&0&0&3&-3&0\end{array}\right)}\xrightarrow{\sigma_{7}\sigma_{5}\sigma_{3}}{\tiny\left(\begin{array}[]{rrrrrrrr}0&-3&0&2&3&3&3&2\\
3&0&6&6&9&6&6&3\\
0&-6&0&0&0&0&0&0\\
-2&-6&0&0&0&0&0&0\\
-3&-9&0&0&0&-3&-3&-3\\
-3&-6&0&0&3&0&0&-1\\
-3&-6&0&0&3&0&0&-3\\
-2&-3&0&0&3&1&3&0\end{array}\right)}=\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime},8}.

As a consequence of the previous two lemmas, we have the following proposition:

Proposition 3.16
.

We have an isomorphism of
\mathbb{A}
-algebras

(3.34)

\displaystyle\zeta_{k}^{*}(=\mu_{k}^{*}\mu_{k+1}^{*}\mu_{k+2}^{*}\mu_{k}^{*}\mu_{k+3}^{*}\mu_{k+1}^{*}\mu_{k}^{*}\mu_{k+2}^{*}\mu_{k+3}^{*}\mu_{k}^{*}\sigma_{k}^{*}\sigma_{k+2}^{*}\sigma_{k+4}^{*})\colon\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}

given by

Z_{u}\mapsto\mu_{k}^{*}\mu_{k+1}^{*}\mu_{k+2}^{*}\mu_{k}^{*}\mu_{k+3}^{*}\mu_{k+1}^{*}\mu_{k}^{*}\mu_{k+2}^{*}\mu_{k+3}^{*}\mu_{k}^{*}(Z_{\sigma_{k+4}\sigma_{k+2}\sigma_{k}(u)})\quad\text{for all $u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}$},

which induces a bijection between the sets of quantum cluster monomials.

Remark 3.17
.

There are another
31
sequences of mutation of length
10
, which give rise to mutation equivalence from
\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}
. For instance, we have

\mu_{k+3}^{*}\mu_{k+2}^{*}\mu_{k+1}^{*}\mu_{k+3}^{*}\mu_{k}^{*}\mu_{k+2}^{*}\mu_{k+3}^{*}\mu_{k+1}^{*}\mu_{k}^{*}\mu_{k+3}^{*}\sigma_{k}^{*}\sigma_{k+2}^{*}\sigma_{k+4}^{*}\colon\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}.

In Appendix
B
, we record all the
32
sequences of mutations of length
10
that produce the equivalence with index permutation
\sigma_{k}^{*}\sigma_{k+2}^{*}\sigma_{k+4}^{*}
. Recall that
\mathbf{deg}
does not depend on
the choice of sequences of mutations
[
50
,
17
]
.

Lemma 3.18
.

If
\mathsf{z}\in\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}
is a quantum cluster monomial with
\mathbf{deg}(\mathsf{z})={\bm{g}}^{\prime}=(g^{\prime}_{u})_{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
, then the quantum cluster monomial

\zeta_{k}^{*}\mathsf{z}\in\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}
has
\mathbf{deg}(\zeta_{k}^{*}\mathsf{z})={\bm{g}}=(g_{u})_{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
as follows:

(3.35)

\displaystyle g_{j}=\begin{cases}g^{\prime}_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}+[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+[B]_{+}-[-G]_{+}-[-I]_{+}&\text{ if }j=k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},\\
g^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}+[D]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+2[H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[I]_{+}&\text{ if }j=(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-},\\
-I&\text{ if }j=k,\\
-H+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-I]_{+}&\text{ if }j=k+1,\\
-G+[I]_{+}&\text{ if }j=k+2,\\
-E+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[H]_{+}&\text{ if }j=k+3,\\
g^{\prime}_{k+5}-[-B]_{+}+[G]_{+}&\text{ if }j=k+4,\\
g^{\prime}_{k+4}-[-A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[E]_{+}&\text{ if }j=k+5,\\
g^{\prime}_{j}&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}

where

(3.36)

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}g^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+},\qquad B\mathbin{:=}g^{\prime}_{k+3}-[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+},\qquad C\mathbin{:=}-g^{\prime}_{k+1}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[A]_{+}-[-B]_{+},

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}g^{\prime}_{k}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-2[-A]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+},\ \qquad\qquad E\mathbin{:=}-A-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[C]_{+}+[D]_{+},

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}-C+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-D]_{+},\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ G\mathbin{:=}-B-[-C]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-E]_{+}+[F]_{+},

\displaystyle H

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}-D+[E]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-F]_{+},\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ I\mathbin{:=}-F+[G]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-H]_{+}.

Proof.

By applying (
2.4
) repeatedly,
we can obtain the assertion.
∎

Lemma 3.19
.

The map
{\bm{g}}^{\prime}\mapsto{\bm{g}}
given by (
3.35
) sends the cone
C_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}\subset\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}^{\oplus\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
into the cone
C_{\textbf{{i}}}\subset\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}^{\oplus\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
.

Proof.

Let us use similar arguments to those in Lemma
3.10
and notations in (
3.36
).

(a) If
u>k+5
, we have
\Sigma_{u}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{u}\geqslant 0
.

(b) If
u=k+5
, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+5}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-[-A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[E]_{+}.

Note that if
A<0
, then
E>0
and
A+E\geqslant 0
since

\displaystyle A+E

\displaystyle=A+(-A-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[C]_{+}+[D]_{+})=-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[C]_{+}+[D]_{+}\geqslant 0.

Thus
\Sigma_{k+5}\geqslant 0
.
Assume that
A\geqslant 0
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+5}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[E]_{+},

which proves the assertion for
k+5
.

(c) If
u=k+4
, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+5}^{\prime}-[-B]_{+}+[G]_{+}.

Thus it suffices to consider when
B<0
. If
g^{\prime}_{k+1}<0
, then

\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+5}+B=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+1}\geqslant 0
, and if
g^{\prime}_{k+1}\geqslant 0
, then

\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+5}+B=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k+3}\geqslant 0
. Thus the assertion follows.

(d) If
u=k+3
, we have

(3.37)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+3}=

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-[-A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[E]_{+}-E+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[H]_{+}.

Applying
[E]_{+}-E=[-E]_{+}
, (
3.37
) becomes

(3.38)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+3}=

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-[-A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[-E]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[H]_{+}.

(
A<0,E>0
)
: In this case, we have

[-E]_{+}=0,\quad-[-A]_{+}=A=g^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}

and hence (
3.38
) becomes

\displaystyle\underline{\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k+2}}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[H]_{+}

\displaystyle=\underline{\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[H]_{+}.

Then it is enough to consider when

0>G=-B-[-C]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-E]_{+}+[F]_{+}.

Note that it suffices to show that

(3.39)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}\geqslant 0.

Replacing
G
with the above formula, (
3.38
) turns into

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(-B-[-C]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-E]_{+}+[F]_{+})

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}([B]_{+}-B)+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}-3[-E]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}

\displaystyle\overset{*}{=}\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}.

Here
\overset{*}{=}
follows from

(1)
E\geqslant 0
by the assumption

and

(2)
[B]_{+}-B=[-B]_{+}
.

Finally assume

0>C=-g^{\prime}_{k+1}-[-B]_{+}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}

\displaystyle\hskip 43.05542pt\overset{\dagger}{=}\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}.

Here
\overset{\dagger}{=}
follows from
(3)
[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-g^{\prime}_{k+1}=[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}
and
(4)
[-B]_{+}-[-B]_{+}=0
.
Then our assertion for this case follows.

(
A\geqslant 0,E<0
)
: In this case, we have

[-E]_{+}=-E,\quad-[-A]_{+}=0

and hence (
3.38
) becomes

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}-E+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[H]_{+}

Since
-E>0
, it suffices to consider when

0>G=-B-[-C]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}E+[F]_{+},

as in the previous case.
Replacing
G
with the above formula, (
3.38
) turns into

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}-E+[H]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}B+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}+3E-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}

\displaystyle\overset{\ddagger}{=}\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}+2E+[H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}.

Here
\overset{\ddagger}{=}
follows from (1)
[B]_{+}-B=[-B]_{+}
. Finally assume

0>C=-g^{\prime}_{k+1}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}A-[-B]_{+}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}+2E+[H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}+3A+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}

\displaystyle\overset{\star}{=}\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-2A+2[D]_{+}+[H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}+3A

\displaystyle\overset{\sharp}{=}\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+2[D]_{+}+[H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\diamond}{=}\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+2[D]_{+}+[H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}.

Here

(2)

\overset{\star}{=}
follows from the replacement of
E
with
-A+[D]_{+}
,

(3)

\overset{\sharp}{=}
follows from the replacement of
A
with
g^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}
,

(4)

\overset{\diamond}{=}
follows from
\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k+2}=\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}
and
[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-g^{\prime}_{k+1}=[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}
.

(
A\geqslant 0,E\geqslant 0
)
: In this case,
we have

[-E]_{+}=0,\quad-[-A]_{+}=0

and hence (
3.38
) becomes

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[H]_{+}.

Similarly, it suffices to consider when

0>G=-B-[-C]_{+}+[F]_{+}.

Replacing
G
with the above formula, (
3.38
) turns into

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[H]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}B+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}+[H]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}.

Finally assume

0>C=-g^{\prime}_{k+1}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}A-[-B]_{+}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}+[H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}+3A+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}

\displaystyle\overset{\wedge}{=}\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+[H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}+2A+g^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}

\displaystyle\overset{\oplus}{=}\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}+[H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+2A\geqslant 0,

which completes a proof of the assertion for
k+3
.
Here

(i)

\overset{\wedge}{=}
follows from
[-B]_{+}-[-B]_{+}=0
and
A=g^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}
,

(ii)

\overset{\oplus}{=}
follows from
\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k+2}
and
[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-g^{\prime}_{k+1}=[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}
.

(e) If
u=k+2
, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+2}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+5}^{\prime}-[-B]_{+}+[G]_{+}-G+[I]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+5}^{\prime}-[-B]_{+}+[-G]_{+}+[I]_{+}.

Thus the assertion for
k+2
is a direct consequence of the assertion for
k+4
.

(f) If
u=k+1
, we have

(3.40)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-[-A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[-E]_{+}

\displaystyle+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+\underline{[H]_{+}-H}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-I]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-[-A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[-E]_{+}

\displaystyle+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+\underline{[-H]_{+}}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-I]_{+}.

If
I\geqslant 0
, the assertion for
k+1
follows from the one for
k+3
. Thus it suffices to assume that
I<0
.

(
A<0,E\geqslant 0,I<0
)
: By replacing
I
with
-F+[G]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-H]_{+}
, (
3.40
)
becomes

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+A-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[-H]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}F-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[G]_{+}-3[-H]_{+}

\displaystyle\overset{\square}{=}\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(F-G)-2[-H]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(F-G)-2[-H]_{+}.

Here
\overset{\square}{=}
follows from
-[-G]_{+}+[G]_{+}=G
.
Note that the assumption
I<0
is equivalent to

(3.41)

\displaystyle[G]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-H]_{+}<F.

Using (
3.41
), we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(F-G)-2[-H]_{+}

\displaystyle>\mathsf{c}_{j,i}([G]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-H]_{+}-G)-2[-H]_{+}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[-H]_{+}=\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[-H]_{+}.

Then

(3.42)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+1}>\left\{\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}\right\}+[-H]_{+}.

Then (
3.39
) implies that the
\{\}
-part in (
3.42
) is non-negative and hence our assertion for this case follows.

(
A\geqslant 0,E<0,I<0
)
: By replacing
I
with
-F+[G]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-H]_{+}
, (
3.40
)
becomes

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}-E+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[-H]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}F-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[G]_{+}-3[-H]_{+}

(3.43)

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}-E+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(F-G)-2[-H]_{+}

as in the former case. Here we remark that
-E>0
.

Using (
3.41
) again and (
3.43
), we have an inequality

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(F-G)-2[-H]_{+}-E

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt>\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}-E+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}([G]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-H]_{+}-G)-2[-H]_{+}

(3.44)

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[-H]_{+}-E.

Now let us assume that

0>G=-B-[-C]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-E]_{+}+[F]_{+}.

Then (
3.44
) becomes

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[-H]_{+}-E+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}B+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}+3E-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}

\displaystyle\overset{\triangle}{=}\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}+[-H]_{+}+2E+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{=}\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}+[-H]_{+}-2A-2\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[C]_{+}+[D]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}

\displaystyle\overset{\bullet}{=}\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}+[-H]_{+}-2A-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[C]_{+}+[D]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}C-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}

\displaystyle\overset{*}{=}\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}+[-H]_{+}-2A-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[C]_{+}

\displaystyle\hskip 150.69397pt-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+[D]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}+3A+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}

\displaystyle\overset{\nabla}{=}\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+[-H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[C]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+[D]_{+}+A+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\odot}{=}\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+[-H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[C]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+[D]_{+}+g^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\vee}{=}\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}+[-H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[C]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+[D]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}.

Here

(i)

\overset{\triangle}{=}
follows from
[B]_{+}-B=[-B]_{+}
,

(ii)

\overset{\circ}{=}
follows from
E=-A-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[C]_{+}+[D]_{+}
,

(iii)

\overset{\bullet}{=}
follows from
[C]_{+}-[-C]_{+}=C
,

(iv)

\overset{*}{=}
follows from
C=-g^{\prime}_{k+1}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[A]_{+}-[-B]_{+}
,

(v)

\overset{\nabla}{=}
follows from
[-B]_{+}-[-B]_{+}=0
,

(vi)

\overset{\odot}{=}
follows from
A=g^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}
,

(vii)

\overset{\vee}{=}
follows from
\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k+2}
and
[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-g^{\prime}_{k+1}=[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}
.

(
A\geqslant 0,E\geqslant 0,I<0
)
: By replacing
I
with
-F+[G]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{i,j}[-H]_{+}
, (
3.40
)
becomes

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[-H]_{+}

\displaystyle\hskip 107.63855pt+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}F-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[G]_{+}-3[-H]_{+}

(3.45)

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}-2[-H]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(F-G)

as in the previous cases. Using (
3.41
) and (
3.45
), we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}-2[-H]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}(F-G)

(3.46)

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt>\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[-H]_{+}.

Now let us assume that

0>G=-B-[-C]_{+}+[F]_{+}.

Then (
3.46
) becomes

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[-H]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}B+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}+[-H]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}

Finally, it suffices to assume that

0>C=-g^{\prime}_{k+1}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}A-[-B]_{+}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}+[-H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}+3A+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+[-H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+2A+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}+g^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+2}^{\prime}+[-H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+2A-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}.

Since
A\geqslant 0
, it proves the assertion for
k+1
.

(g) If
u=k
, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+5}^{\prime}-[-B]_{+}+[G]_{+}-G+[I]_{+}-I

(3.47)

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+5}^{\prime}-[-B]_{+}+[-G]_{+}+[-I]_{+}.

Thus the assertion for
k
is also a direct consequence of the assertion for

.

(h) For
u<k
, the assertion follows from Lemma
3.20
below.
∎

Lemma 3.20
.

Assume that
k^{-}\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}
. Then we have

\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{g}}^{\prime};i)=\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{g}};i)\quad\text{ for all }i\in I,

where
{\bm{g}}^{\prime}\mapsto{\bm{g}}
under the map given by (
3.35
).

Proof.

It suffices to show that

(i)

g_{k+5}+g_{k+3}+g_{k+1}+g_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}=g^{\prime}_{k+4}+g^{\prime}_{k+2}+g^{\prime}_{k}+g^{\prime}_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}}
,

(ii)

g_{k+4}+g_{k+2}+g_{k}+g_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}=g^{\prime}_{k+5}+g^{\prime}_{k+3}+g^{\prime}_{k+1}+g^{\prime}_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}
.

(
i
)
: By (
3.40
) and applying techniques what we have been used,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}-[-A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[-E]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[-H]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-I]_{+}+[D]_{+}

\displaystyle\hskip 43.05542pt-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+2[H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[I]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}-[-A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[-E]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt+[-H]_{+}+[D]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+2[H]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}I

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}-[-A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[-E]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-G]_{+}+[-H]_{+}+[D]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt+2[H]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}F-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[G]_{+}-3[-H]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}-[-A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[-E]_{+}+[D]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}+2H+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}F-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}G

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}-[-A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[-E]_{+}+[D]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}-2D+2[E]_{+}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt+2\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-F]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}C+3[-D]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}B+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}-3[-E]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[F]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}-[-A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[D]_{+}+3[-D]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}-2D+2E

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt-2\mathsf{c}_{j,i}F-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}C+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}B

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}-[-A]_{+}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[B]_{+}+[D]_{+}+3[-D]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}-2g^{\prime}_{k}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt-2\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+4[-A]_{+}-2\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}-2A-2\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[C]_{+}+2[D]_{+}+2\mathsf{c}_{j,i}C

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt-6[-D]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}+3[A]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-B]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+3}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}-2g^{\prime}_{k}-3\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+3[-A]_{+}-3\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}+3[A]_{+}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+3}-2A-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}B+3D

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}-2g^{\prime}_{k}-3\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+3[-A]_{+}-3\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}+3[A]_{+}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+3}-2A-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}B+3g^{\prime}_{k}+3\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}-6[-A]_{+}+3\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-C]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}+g^{\prime}_{k}+A+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+3}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}B

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}+g^{\prime}_{k}+g^{\prime}_{k+2}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+1}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+3}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}g^{\prime}_{k+3}+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+4}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}+g^{\prime}_{k}+g^{\prime}_{k+2}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{k_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}

(
ii
)
: By (
3.47
), we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+5}^{\prime}-[-B]_{+}+[-G]_{+}+[-I]_{+}+g^{\prime}_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}+[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+[B]_{+}-[-G]_{+}-[-I]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+5}^{\prime}+B+g^{\prime}_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}+[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+5}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k+3}-[-g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+[g^{\prime}_{k+1}]_{+}+g^{\prime}_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{k+5}^{\prime}+g^{\prime}_{k+3}+g^{\prime}_{k+1}+g^{\prime}_{(k+1)_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-}}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}}.\qed

The following lemma can be obtained as in other moves and we omit the proof.

Lemma 3.21
.

Let
{\bm{g}},{\bm{g}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}^{\oplus\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
be elements related by (
3.15
). If
{\bm{g}}^{\prime}_{s}=0
for
s\in[k,k+3]
, we have

\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{g}};i)=\mathsf{p}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{g}}^{\prime};i)\qquad\text{ for any }i\in I.

Example 3.22
.

Take
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=(2,1,2,1,2,1,\ldots)
and
\textbf{{i}}=(1,2,1,2,1,2,\ldots)
. Using the above formulae, the map
\uptheta_{1}^{(1)*}:{\bm{g}}^{\prime}\mapsto{\bm{g}}
in (
3.35
) that is induced by the isomorphism
\zeta_{1}^{(1)*}:\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}
can be descried for special
{\bm{g}}
’s as follows:

(3.48)

\displaystyle\uptheta_{1}^{(1)*}({\bm{e}}_{1})={\bm{e}}_{6}-{\bm{e}}_{4},

\displaystyle\uptheta_{1}^{(1)*}({\bm{e}}_{2})=({\bm{e}}_{6}-{\bm{e}}_{4})+3{\bm{e}}_{1},

\displaystyle\uptheta_{1}^{(1)*}({\bm{e}}_{3}-{\bm{e}}_{1})=2({\bm{e}}_{6}-{\bm{e}}_{4})+3{\bm{e}}_{1},

\displaystyle\uptheta_{1}^{(1)*}({\bm{e}}_{4}-{\bm{e}}_{2})=({\bm{e}}_{6}-{\bm{e}}_{4})+2{\bm{e}}_{1},

\displaystyle\uptheta_{1}^{(1)*}({\bm{e}}_{5}-{\bm{e}}_{3})=({\bm{e}}_{6}-{\bm{e}}_{4})+3{\bm{e}}_{1},

\displaystyle\uptheta_{1}^{(1)*}({\bm{e}}_{6}-{\bm{e}}_{4})={\bm{e}}_{1}.

Similarly, the map
\uptheta_{1}^{(2)*}
induced by the isomorphism
\zeta_{1}^{(2)*}:\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}
can be descried as follows:

(3.49)

\displaystyle\uptheta_{1}^{(2)*}({\bm{e}}_{1})=({\bm{e}}_{6}-{\bm{e}}_{4}),

\displaystyle\uptheta_{1}^{(2)*}({\bm{e}}_{2})=3({\bm{e}}_{6}-{\bm{e}}_{4})+{\bm{e}}_{1},

\displaystyle\uptheta_{1}^{(2)*}({\bm{e}}_{3}-{\bm{e}}_{1})=2({\bm{e}}_{6}-{\bm{e}}_{4})+{\bm{e}}_{1},

\displaystyle\uptheta_{1}^{(2)*}({\bm{e}}_{4}-{\bm{e}}_{2})=3({\bm{e}}_{6}-{\bm{e}}_{4})+2{\bm{e}}_{1},

\displaystyle\uptheta_{1}^{(2)*}({\bm{e}}_{5}-{\bm{e}}_{3})=({\bm{e}}_{6}-{\bm{e}}_{4})+{\bm{e}}_{1},

\displaystyle\uptheta_{1}^{(2)*}({\bm{e}}_{6}-{\bm{e}}_{4})={\bm{e}}_{1}.

3.7.
Forward shifts

In this subsection, we consider a forward shift of
\textbf{{i}}\in I^{\mathbb{N}}\mspace{1mu}

introduced in
[
11
]
when
\mathfrak{g}
is of simply-laced type. However, in this subsection,
we do not restrict types of
\mathfrak{g}
.

We say that
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=(i^{\prime}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
is obtained from
\textbf{{i}}=(i_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
via a forward shift if

i^{\prime}_{k}=i_{k+1}\quad\text{for all $k\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}$}.

In this case, we write
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=\partial_{+}\textbf{{i}}
.
Throughout this subsection, we assume that
\textbf{{i}},\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}\in I^{(\infty)}
are related by
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=\partial_{+}\textbf{{i}}
and
i_{1}\mathbin{:=}i
. Define

(a)

a sequence
(x_{u})_{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
such that

x_{1}=1
and
x_{u}=(x_{u-1})_{\textbf{{i}}}^{+}
for
u\geqslant 2
,

(b)

a permutation
\sigma_{+}\in\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbb{N}}\mspace{1mu}
by

\sigma_{+}(k)=\begin{cases}k_{\textbf{{i}}}^{+}-1&\text{ if }i_{k}=i,\\
k-1&\text{ if }i_{k}\neq i.\end{cases}

Remark 3.23
.

Even though
[
11
]
considered only simply-laced types, the statements and proofs in
[
11
, §2.3]
can be extended to non simply-laced types. Thus we only give
the statements and leave their proofs to the reader.

Proposition 3.24
.

(a)

The limit

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mu_{x_{n}}\cdots\mu_{x_{2}}\mu_{x_{1}}\widetilde{B}^{\textbf{{i}}}

yields a well-defined matrix
\mu_{+}\widetilde{B}^{\textbf{{i}}}
and we have
\sigma_{+}\mu_{+}\widetilde{B}^{\textbf{{i}}}=\widetilde{B}^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}
.

(b)

The limit

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mu_{x_{n}}\cdots\mu_{x_{2}}\mu_{x_{1}}\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}}

yields a well-defined matrix
\mu_{+}\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}}
and we have
\sigma_{+}\mu_{+}\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}}=\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}
.

(c)

We have a homomorphism of
\mathbb{A}
-algebras

\partial_{+}^{*}(=\mu_{+}^{*}\sigma_{+}^{*})\colon\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}\qquad\text{given by $Z_{u}\mapsto\mu_{+}(Z_{\sigma_{+}^{-1}(u)})$ for all $u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}$},

which sends each cluster monomial in
\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}
to a cluster monomial in
\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}
.

(d)

If
\mathsf{z}\in\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}
is a quantum cluster monomial with
\mathbf{deg}(\mathsf{z})={\bm{g}}^{\prime}=(g^{\prime}_{u})_{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
, then the quantum cluster monomial

\partial_{+}^{*}\mathsf{z}\in\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}
has
\mathbf{deg}(\partial_{+}^{*}\mathsf{z})={\bm{g}}=(g_{u})_{u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}}
as follows:

(3.50)

g_{u}=\begin{cases}-\sum_{v\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu};i^{\prime}_{v}=i_{1}}g^{\prime}_{v}&\text{if $u=1$},\\
g^{\prime}_{u-1}&\text{if $u>1$}.\\
\end{cases}

In other words, the assignment
{\bm{g}}^{\prime}\mapsto{\bm{g}}
is given by the
\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}_{0}
-linear map
C_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}\to C_{\textbf{{i}}}
which sends
{\bm{e}}_{u}-{\bm{e}}_{u^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}}\in C_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}
to
{\bm{e}}_{u+1}-{\bm{e}}_{(u+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}\in C_{\textbf{{i}}}
for all
u\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}
.

4.
Bases and braid moves

In this section, we first recall the quantum unipotent coordinate algebra

\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})
of
\mathfrak{g}
and its bases.
Then we apply the result in the previous section to investigate the relationship among braid moves and the bases.

4.1.
Quantum unipotent coordinate algebra

Let
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
be the quantum group of
\mathfrak{g}
over
\mathbb{Q}(q^{1/2})
, generated by
e_{i},f_{i}

(i\in I)

and
q^{h}
, where
h
is an element in the dual weight lattice
\mathsf{P}^{\vee}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mspace{1mu}(\mathsf{P},\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu})
. We denote by

\mathcal{U}_{q}^{+}(\mathfrak{g})
the subalgebra of
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
generated by
e_{i}

(i\in I)
and

\mathcal{U}_{\mathbb{A}}^{+}(\mathfrak{g})
the
\mathbb{A}
-subalgebra of
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{+}(\mathfrak{g})
generated by

e_{i}/[n]_{i}!
, where

q_{i}\mathbin{:=}q^{\mathsf{d}_{i}},\ \ \ [k]_{i}\mathbin{:=}\dfrac{q_{i}^{k}-q_{i}^{-k}}{q_{i}-q_{i}^{-1}},\ \ \text{ and }\ \ [k]_{i}!\mathbin{:=}\prod_{s=1}^{k}[s_{i}]\ \ \text{ for }k\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}.

Set

\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})=\mathop{\mbox{\normalsize$\bigoplus$}}\limits_{{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\mathsf{Q}^{-}}\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})_{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\quad\text{ where }\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})_{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\mathbin{:=}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{Q}(q^{1/2})}(\mathcal{U}^{+}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})_{-{\mspace{1mu}\beta}},\mathbb{Q}(q^{1/2})),

where
\mathcal{U}^{+}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})_{-{\mspace{1mu}\beta}}
denotes the
(-{\mspace{1mu}\beta})
-root space of

\mathcal{U}^{+}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
. Then
\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})
has an algebra structure and
is called the
quantum unipotent coordinate algebra
of
\mathfrak{g}
.
We denote by
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
the
\mathbb{A}
-submodule of
\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})

generated by
\uppsi\in\mathcal{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{n})
such that
\uppsi(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbb{A}}^{+}(\mathfrak{g}))\subset\mathbb{A}
. Then
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
also has an
\mathbb{A}
-algebra structure.

For each
\lambda\in\mathsf{P}^{+}\mathbin{:=}\sum_{i\in I}\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}\varpi_{i}
and Weyl group elements

w,w^{\prime}\in\mathsf{W}
, we can define a specific homogeneous element
D(w\lambda,w^{\prime}\lambda)

of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
, called a
quantum unipotent minor
(see for example
[
29
, Section 9]
). Note that
D(w\lambda,w^{\prime}\lambda)
vanishes if
w\lambda-w^{\prime}\lambda\not\in\mathsf{Q}^{-}
. We set

\widetilde{D}(w\lambda,w^{\prime}\lambda)\mathbin{:=}q^{-(w^{\prime}\lambda-w\lambda,w^{\prime}\lambda-w\lambda)/4-(w^{\prime}\lambda-w\lambda,\rho)/2}D(w\lambda,w^{\prime}\lambda)

where
\rho\mathbin{:=}\sum_{i\in I}\varpi_{i}\in\mathsf{P}^{+}
, and call it a
normalized quantum unipotent minor
.

Let us fix a reduced sequence
i
of
w_{\circ}
. For

1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell
, we set

\widetilde{F}_{\textbf{{i}}}(k)\mathbin{:=}\widetilde{D}(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k}\varpi_{i_{k}},w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k^{-}}\varpi_{i_{k}}).

Note that
{\rm wt}(\widetilde{F}_{\textbf{{i}}}(k))=-{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k}\in\Phi^{-}
. For
{\bm{c}}=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{\ell})\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}_{0}^{\ell}
,
we set

\nu({\bm{c}})=-\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{1\leqslant k,l\leqslant\ell}c_{k}c_{l}({\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k},{\mspace{1mu}\beta}^{\textbf{{i}}}_{l})+\sum_{s=1}^{\ell}c_{s}^{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}

and define

\widetilde{F}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}})\mathbin{:=}q^{\nu({\bm{c}})/2}\prod^{\to}_{1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell}\widetilde{F}_{\textbf{{i}}}(k)^{c_{k}}.

Proposition 4.1
.

(
see, for instance,
[
46
, Chapter 40,41]
)

The set
\{\widetilde{F}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}})\ |\ {\bm{c}}\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}_{0}^{\ell}\}
forms an
\mathbb{A}
-basis of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
.

There exist an anti-involution
\overline{(\cdot)}
on
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})

defined by

q^{\pm 1/2}\longmapsto q^{\mp 1/2}\quad\text{ and }\quad\widetilde{F}_{\textbf{{i}}}(k)\longmapsto\widetilde{F}_{\textbf{{i}}}(k)

for all
1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell
, which is called the
twisted bar-involution
.

4.2.
Comparison with braid moves

Lusztig
[
44
,
45
]
and Kashiwara
[
30
]
have constructed a distinguished
\mathbb{A}
-basis
\mathbf{B}^{\rm up}
, which is called the
dual-canonical/upper-global basis
.
In this paper we consider the normalized one
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}
of
\mathbf{B}^{\rm up}
(see
[
43
]
for more details).
Note that
\widetilde{D}(w\lambda,w^{\prime}\lambda)\in\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}
if it is non-zero.

Theorem 4.2

(
[
43
, Theorem 4.29]
)
.

For a reduced sequence
i
of
w_{\circ}
, we have

\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}=\{\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}})\ |\ {\bm{c}}\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}_{0}^{\ell}\}

where
\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}})
is the unique element of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
satisfying the following properties for each
{\bm{c}}\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}_{0}^{\Phi^{+}}
:

(a)

\overline{\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}})}=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}})
,

(b)

\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}})-\widetilde{F}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}})\in\sum_{{\bm{c}}^{\prime}<_{\textbf{{i}}}{\bm{c}}}q\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q]\widetilde{F}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}}^{\prime})
,

where
<_{\textbf{{i}}}
is a certain bi-lexicographical order determined by

i
. In particular,
\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{k})=\widetilde{D}(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k}\varpi_{i_{k}},w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{k^{-}}\varpi_{i_{k}})
.

Proposition 4.3

(
[
46
, Chapter 42]
,
[
1
, Theorem 3.1]
)
.

Let
i
and
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}
be two reduced sequences for
w_{\circ}
, and
{\bm{c}}=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{\ell}),{\bm{c}}^{\prime}=(c^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,c^{\prime}_{\ell})\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}_{0}^{\ell}
.

(1)

Assume
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=\gamma_{k}\textbf{{i}}
for some
1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell-1
. Then we have

\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{c}})\quad\text{ if and only if }\quad\begin{cases}(c_{k},c_{k+1})=(c^{\prime}_{k+1},c^{\prime}_{k}),\\
c_{u}=c^{\prime}_{u}\text{ if }u\not\in\{k,k+1\}.\end{cases}

(2)

Assume
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=\beta_{k}\textbf{{i}}
for some
1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell-2
. Then we have

\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{c}})\quad\text{ if and only if }\quad\begin{cases}c^{\prime}_{k}=c_{k+1}+c_{k+2}-\min(c_{k},c_{k+2}),\\
c^{\prime}_{k+1}=\min(c_{k},c_{k+2}),\\
c^{\prime}_{k+2}=c_{k+1}+c_{k}-\min(c_{k},c_{k+2}),\\
c_{u}=c^{\prime}_{u}\text{ if }u\not\in\{k,k+1,k+2\}.\end{cases}

(3)

Assume
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=\eta_{k}\textbf{{i}}
for some
1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell-3
. Note that we can regard
(i_{k},i_{k+1},i_{k+2},i_{k+3})
and

(i^{\prime}_{k},i^{\prime}_{k+1},i^{\prime}_{k+2},i^{\prime}_{k+3})
as reduced sequences of
w_{\circ}
of type
B_{2}
,
and hence we have parametrizations of these sequences in terms of
\Phi^{+}_{B_{2}}

(
see
Example
3.1
)
.
Then we have

\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{c}}^{\prime})\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\begin{cases}c^{\prime}_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}}=c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}}+2c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}}+c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}}-\pi_{2},\\
c^{\prime}_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}}=\pi_{2}-\pi_{1},\\
c^{\prime}_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}}=2\pi_{1}-\pi_{2},\\
c^{\prime}_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}}=c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}}+c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}}+c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}}-\pi_{1},\\
c^{\prime}_{u}=c_{u}\text{ if $u\not\in\{k,k+1,k+2,k+3\}$},\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle\pi_{1}=\min\{c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}}+c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}},c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}}+c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}},c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}}+c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}}\},

\displaystyle\pi_{2}=\min\{2c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}}+c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}},2c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}}+c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}},2c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{1}+2{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}}+c_{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}_{2}}\}.

(4)

For
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=(2,1,2,1,2,1)
and
\textbf{{i}}=(1,2,1,2,1,2)
of
G_{2}
-type, we have

(4.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{1})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{6}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{2})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{1}+{\bm{e}}_{6}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{3})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}(3{\bm{e}}_{1}+2{\bm{e}}_{6}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{4})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}(2{\bm{e}}_{1}+3{\bm{e}}_{6}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{5})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}(3{\bm{e}}_{1}+{\bm{e}}_{6}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{6})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{1}),

and

(4.2)

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{1})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{6}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{2})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{1}+3{\bm{e}}_{6}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{3})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{1}+2{\bm{e}}_{6}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{4})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}(2{\bm{e}}_{1}+3{\bm{e}}_{6}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{5})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{6}+{\bm{e}}_{1}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{6})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{1}).

Theorem 4.4

(
[
13
,
15
,
29
,
31
,
16
,
51
,
37
]
)
.

For each reduced sequence
\textbf{{i}}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell})
of
w_{\circ}
, there is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{A}
-algebras

\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}\colon\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})

which satisfies the following properties:

(1)

For any
1\leqslant v<u\leqslant\ell
with
i_{u}=i_{v}
, there exists a cluster variable which corresponds to
\widetilde{D}(w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{u}\varpi_{i_{u}},w^{\textbf{{i}}}_{v}\varpi_{i_{v}})
under
\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}
.

(2)

The initial seed of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})
corresponding to that of
\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}
is given by

(4.3)

\displaystyle\big(\{\widetilde{D}(w_{k}^{\textbf{{i}}}\varpi_{i_{k}},\varpi_{i_{k}})\}_{1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell},\Lambda^{\textbf{{i}}},\widetilde{B}^{\textbf{{i}}}\big).

(3)

Every cluster monomial of
\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}
corresponds to an element of the basis
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}
under
\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}
.

(4)

For each
{\bm{c}}=(c_{u})\in\mathbb{N}\mspace{1mu}_{0}^{\ell}
, the element
\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}})
is pointed and we have

(4.4)

\mathbf{deg}\big(\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{c}})\big)=\sum_{1\leqslant u\leqslant\ell}c_{u}({\bm{e}}_{u}-{\bm{e}}_{u^{-}})\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}^{\ell},

where we understand
{\bm{e}}_{0}=0
.
In particular, the map
\mathbf{deg}\circ\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-1}\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}\to\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}^{\ell}
is injective.

The following proposition is proved for
2
-moves and
3
-moves in
[
11
, Proposition 3.3]
, and we extend it to
4
-moves and
6
-moves:

Proposition 4.5
.

Let
\textbf{{i}},\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}
be two reduced sequences for
w_{\circ}
.
Assume that we have
\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=\tau\textbf{{i}}
for some
\tau\in\{\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{\ell-1}\}\sqcup\{\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{\ell-2}\}\sqcup\{\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{\ell-3}\}\cup\{\zeta_{1}\}
.
Then the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\tau^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})}

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{A}}(\mathfrak{n})

is generated by
\{\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{u})\ |\ 1\leqslant u\leqslant\ell\}
,
it is enough to show that

(4.5)

\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}\tau^{*}\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{u})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{u})

for all
u\in[1,\ell]
.
By Theorem
4.4
and the fact that
\tau^{*}
preserves cluster monomials,
we have

\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}\tau^{*}\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{u})\in\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\rm up}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbf{deg}\big(\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{u})\big)=({\bm{e}}_{u}-{\bm{e}}_{u^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}}).

(
\eta
)
: Let us consider
\tau=\eta_{k}
for some
1\leqslant u\leqslant\ell-2
.
Recall
A
and
B
in (
3.16
).

(i)
u=k+3
. In this case

\mathbf{deg}\big(\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{k+3})\big)=({\bm{e}}_{k+3}-{\bm{e}}_{k+1}),\quad A=\mathsf{c}_{j,i}<0\quad\text{ and }B=-1.

Then we have

g_{k}=-1+\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\mathsf{c}_{i,j}=1
,
g_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}=-1
,
g_{k+3}=g_{k+2}=g_{k+1}=g_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}=0
by (
3.15
).
Hence

\mathbf{deg}\big(\eta_{k}^{*}\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{k+3})\big)={\bm{e}}_{k}-{\bm{e}}_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}=\mathbf{deg}\big(\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{k})\big).

(ii)
u=k+2
. In this case

\mathbf{deg}\big(\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{k+2})\big)={\bm{e}}_{k+2}-{\bm{e}}_{k},\quad A=-1\quad\text{ and }\quad B=\mathsf{c}_{i,j}<0.

Then we have

g_{k}=-g_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}=-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}
,
g_{k+3}=-g_{k+1}=1,

g_{k+2}=g_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}=0
.
Hence

\displaystyle\mathbf{deg}\big(\eta_{k}^{*}\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{k+2})\big)

\displaystyle=({\bm{e}}_{k+3}-{\bm{e}}_{k+1})-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}({\bm{e}}_{k}-{\bm{e}}_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}})=\mathbf{deg}\big(\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}(-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}{\bm{e}}_{k}+{\bm{e}}_{k+3})\big).

(iii)
u=k+1
. In this case

\mathbf{deg}\big(\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{k+1})\big)=({\bm{e}}_{k+1}-{\bm{e}}_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}})=({\bm{e}}_{k+1}-{\bm{e}}_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}),\quad A=0\quad\text{ and }\quad B=-1.

Then we have

g_{k}=-g_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}=1
,
g_{k+3}=-g_{k+1}=-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}
,
g_{k+2}=g_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}=0
.
Hence

\displaystyle\mathbf{deg}\big(\eta_{k}^{*}\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{k+1})\big)

\displaystyle=({\bm{e}}_{k}-{\bm{e}}_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}})-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}({\bm{e}}_{k+3}-{\bm{e}}_{k+1})=\mathbf{deg}\big(\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{k}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}{\bm{e}}_{k+3})\big).

(iv)
u=k
. In this case

\mathbf{deg}\big(\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{k})\big)=({\bm{e}}_{k}-{\bm{e}}_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}})=({\bm{e}}_{k}-{\bm{e}}_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}),

Then we have

g_{k+3}=1
,
g_{k+1}=-1
,
g_{k+2}=g_{k}=g_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}}=g_{(k+1)^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}=g_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}=0
,
by (
3.15
) and (
3.16
).
Hence

\mathbf{deg}\big(\eta_{k}^{*}\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{k})\big)=({\bm{e}}_{k+3}-{\bm{e}}_{k+1})=\big(\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{k+3})\big).

(v)
u\not\in\{k,k+1,k+2,k+3\}
. In this case, one can easily see that

\displaystyle\mathbf{deg}\big(\eta_{k}^{*}\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{u})\big)

\displaystyle=({\bm{e}}_{u}-{\bm{e}}_{u^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}})=\mathbf{deg}\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}({\bm{e}}_{u}).

In conclusion, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{deg}\big(\eta^{*}_{k}\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{u})\big)=\begin{cases}{\bm{e}}_{k}-{\bm{e}}_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}&\text{ if }u=k+3,\\
({\bm{e}}_{k+3}-{\bm{e}}_{k+1})-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}({\bm{e}}_{k}-{\bm{e}}_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}})&\text{ if }u=k+2,\\
({\bm{e}}_{k}-{\bm{e}}_{k^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}})-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}({\bm{e}}_{k+3}-{\bm{e}}_{k+1})&\text{ if }u=k+1,\\
{\bm{e}}_{k+3}-{\bm{e}}_{k+1}&\text{ if }u=k,\\
{\bm{e}}_{u}-{\bm{e}}_{u^{-}_{\textbf{{i}}}}&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}

and get
\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}}\eta^{*}_{k}\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}({\bm{e}}_{u})=\widetilde{G}_{\textbf{{i}}}(\mathbf{c}_{u})
, where

\displaystyle\mathbf{c}_{u}=\begin{cases}{\bm{e}}_{k}&\text{ if }u=k+3,\\
{\bm{e}}_{k+3}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}{\bm{e}}_{k}&\text{ if }u=k+2,\\
{\bm{e}}_{k}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}{\bm{e}}_{k+3}&\text{ if }u=k+1,\\
{\bm{e}}_{k+3}&\text{ if }u=k,\\
{\bm{e}}_{u}&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then our assertion for
\eta_{k}
follows from Proposition
4.3
(
3
).

(
\zeta
)
: The assertion for
\zeta
-case follows from (
3.48
), (
3.49
), (
4.1
), (
4.2
) and Proposition
4.3
(
4
).
∎

Note that any two reduced sequences of
w_{\circ}
are connected by braid moves; i.e.,
there exists a finite sequence
{\bm{\tau}}=(\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{l})
in
\{\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{\ell-1}\}\sqcup\{\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{\ell-2}\}\sqcup\{\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{\ell-3}\}\cup\{\zeta_{1}\}
such that

\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}=\tau_{1}\cdots\tau_{l}\textbf{{i}}
. Then we have the isomorphism

{\bm{\tau}}^{*}=\tau_{l}^{*}\circ\cdots\circ\tau_{1}^{*}:\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}.

Corollary 4.6
.

With the above notation, the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\textbf{{i}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{{\bm{\tau}}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}_{\textbf{{i}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
